## Summary table of LGBTQ rights in the United States

This is simplified for international comparison with other Wikipedia LGBT rights articles.

A checkY denotes that the right exists, while a X markN denotes it does not; a checkY and X markN in the same column means the right varies on a state-by-state basis, or that it varies on the Federal Level.

| LGBTQ right | Federal protection | State-level protection |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Same-sex sexual activity legal | Yes (under *[Lawrence v. Texas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lawrence_v._Texas)*, 2003)[^cite_note-Sodomy_laws_ruled_unconstitutional-744] | Yes |
| Equal age of consent | Yes | Yes |
| [Romeo and Juliet laws](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romeo_and_Juliet_laws) apply equally to same-sex couples as to opposite-sex couples | Yes | Yes/yes |
| Anti-discrimination laws in employment | Yes[^cite_note-745] | Yes/yes |
| Anti-discrimination laws in housing | Yes/yes ([HUD](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Department_of_Housing_and_Urban_Development), a federal agency, changed its interpretation of the Fair Housing Act to prohibit discrimination on the basis of sexual orientation or gender identity. However no law has been passed by congress (or reinterpreted by the supreme court) to ban discrimination on the basis of sexual orientation or gender identity in housing.[^cite_note-746]) | Yes/No |
| Anti-discrimination laws in public accommodations | yes | Yes/yes |
| Anti-discrimination laws in the provision of goods and services | yes | Yes/No[^cite_note-747] |
| Anti-discrimination laws in all other areas | yes | Yes/yes |
| Anti-discrimination laws in health insurance | yes | Yes/yes |
| [Anti-bullying legislation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-bullying_legislation) in schools and colleges | yes | Yes/yes |
| Anti-discrimination laws in schools and colleges | yes | Yes/yes |
| Anti-discrimination laws in [hospitals](18994221.md) | yes | yes |
| Anti-discrimination law in homeless shelters | yes | Yes/yes |
| Discrimination against [Intersex](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Intersex) or [non-binary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Non-binary) persons is interpreted to be a violation of anti-discrimination laws when gender identity and/or sex is enumerated as a protected class | Yes for [non-binary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Non-binary) peopleyes for [Intersex](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Intersex) people | Yes/yes |
| LGBTQ anti-discrimination law in prisons, juvenile halls, and immigration detention centers, including but not limited to transgender people required to be housed according to their gender identity and coverage of transition care | yes | Yes/yes |
| Transgender-disenfranchising voting laws repealed/absent[^cite_note-748] |  | Yes/yes |
| Transgender people allowed to use restrooms and other gender-segregated spaces that correspond to their gender identity |  | Yes/yes |
| Transgender people allowed to participate in the sport of their gender identity | Yes | Yes/yes |
| Single-person restrooms with gender-segregated signage (and a lock) are given new signs saying "All-Gender Restroom" (by policy/law) | yes | Yes/No ([Five states](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bathroom_bill#State_legislation) have enacted laws that mandate all single-user gender-segregated bathrooms be unisex) |
| [Gender confirmation surgery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gender_confirmation_surgery), puberty blockers, hormone replacement therapy and other transition-related healthcare for transgender people required to be covered under health insurance and state [Medicaid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Medicaid) policies | No | Yes/No/ (Some states ban coverage of such medical practices, while others require coverage of it, and others are ambiguous on the issue) |
| LGBTQ education | No | Yes/No (Mixed policies: Multiple states have [Anti-LGBTQ curriculum laws](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-LGBT_curriculum_laws_in_the_United_States) in place that limit instruction and discussion of LGBTQ issues in the classroom,[^cite_note-749] while others, such as [California](5407.md), [Oregon](26811621.md), [Colorado](5399.md), [Illinois](14849.md), and [New Jersey](21648.md) mandate education on LGBTQ issues in school[^cite_note-750]) |
| Hate Crime laws inclusive of Sexual orientation | Yes (Since 2009 under the [Matthew Shepard and James Byrd Jr. Hate Crimes Prevention Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Matthew_Shepard_and_James_Byrd_Jr._Hate_Crimes_Prevention_Act))[^cite_note-751] | Yes/No (35 states enhance sentences for crimes committed against someone on the basis of their sexual orientation, while one state, [Michigan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LGBT_rights_in_Michigan), accounts for Hate Crimes committed on the basis of sexual orientation when collecting data on Hate Crimes) |
| Hate Crime laws inclusive of Gender identity | Yes/No (24 states enhance sentences for crimes committed against some on the basis of them being Transgender or gender non-conforming) |  |
| Ban on police profiling on the basis of both perceived and/or actual gender expression, [gender identity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gender_identity), and sexual orientation | No | Yes/No |
| Sexual orientation allowed as grounds for [asylum](1621432.md) | Yes (Since 1994)[^cite_note-752][^cite_note-753] | Yes |
| Gender identity allowed as grounds for [asylum](1621432.md) | No | No/Yes |
| Prohibition on the detention of immigrants on the basis of their LGBTQ identity. | No | No/Yes |
| Prohibition of deportations of LGBTQ immigrants back to countries with the death penalty for homosexuality | No |  |
| Endorsable visas for immigrants and travelers who are legally recognized as [non-binary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Non-binary) or [Intersex](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Intersex) in their country of origin[^cite_note-754] | No[^cite_note-755] | Yes/No |
| Surrogacy legal for male same-sex couples |  | Yes/No |
| Same-sex marriage | Yes Since 2015 nationwide[^cite_note-Legality_of_Same_Sex_Marriage-756]No [American Samoa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LGBT_rights_in_American_Samoa),[^cite_note-757] [Navajo Nation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LGBT_rights_in_the_Navajo_Nation)[^cite_note-758] and [several other reservations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Same-sex_marriage_in_tribal_nations_in_the_United_States) | Yes |
| Recognition of same-sex couples | Yes[^cite_note-Legality_of_Same_Sex_Marriage-756]No [Navajo Nation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LGBT_rights_in_the_Navajo_Nation) and [several other reservations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Same-sex_marriage_in_tribal_nations_in_the_United_States) | Yes |
| Step-child adoption by same-sex couples | Yes[^cite_note-Adoption-759]No [American Samoa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LGBT_rights_in_American_Samoa), [Navajo Nation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LGBT_rights_in_the_Navajo_Nation) and [several other reservations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Same-sex_marriage_in_tribal_nations_in_the_United_States) | Yes |
| Joint adoption by same-sex couples | Yes[^cite_note-Adoption-759]No [American Samoa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LGBT_rights_in_American_Samoa), [Navajo Nation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LGBT_rights_in_the_Navajo_Nation) and [several other reservations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Same-sex_marriage_in_tribal_nations_in_the_United_States) | Yes |
| Paid family leave for same-sex parents and couples |  | Yes/No[^cite_note-760] |
| LGBTQ anti-discrimination law in adoption, custody, and visitation rights |  | Yes/No |
| [Conjugal visits](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conjugal_visits) for same-sex couples | N/A (conjugal visits banned in federal prisons regardless of sexual orientation) | Yes/No (only in [New York](8210131.md) and [California](5407.md)) |
| LGBTQ people allowed to serve openly in the military | Yes(since [2011](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Don't_Ask,_Don't_Tell_Repeal_Act_of_2010))[^cite_note-Karen_McVeigh_and_Paul_Harris-761] | Yes |
| Transgender people allowed to serve openly in the military | Yes(Banned since 2025)[^cite_note-762][^cite_note-763][^cite_note-764] | Yes |
| Intersex people allowed to serve openly in the military | No (current Department of Defense policy bans "[hermaphrodites](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hermaphrodite#Use_regarding_humans)" from serving or enlisting in the military)[^cite_note-765] |  |
| Right to change legal gender | Yes (Does not require [SRS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sex_reassignment_surgery) for a gender change on passports[^cite_note-766] or [Social Security](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Social_Security_Administration)'s records[^cite_note-767]) | Yes/No (20 states allow a gender change on birth certificates after [SRS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sex_reassignment_surgery), 27 states allow it without being required to undergo such treatments, one state bans all amendments to birth certificates, and two are ambiguous at the moment) |
| Legal recognition of [non-binary gender](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Non-binary_gender) | No | Yes/No |
| Legal recognition of [non-binary gender](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Non-binary_gender) on US passports | Yes (since 2022)[^cite_note-768] | No |
| Abolition of gender-based dress codes which may discriminate against transgender students at schools | No | No/Yes/ (Known cities with such a policy include New York City)[^cite_note-769] |
| Airport security screening gender neutral (body scanning, etc.) | Yes (since 2022) Cessation of "alarm" or "anomaly" security flags on bodily characteristics deemed not congruent, or not to "match" sex designations, as may occur for some transgender persons[^cite_note-770] | No |
| [United States census](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_census) counts number of lesbian, gay, bisexual, and transgender people and recognizes a [non-binary gender](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Non-binary_gender) or [Intersex](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Intersex) option | No | No |
| Intersex minors protected from invasive surgical procedures | No | No |
| Conversion therapy banned on minors | No | Yes/No |
| [Gay panic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gay_panic_defense) and [trans panic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gay_panic_defense) defenses banned by law | No | Yes/No ([20 States and Washington D.C.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gay_panic_defense#United_States) currently ban it.) |
| Gay and Lesbian Criminal records expunged | No | Yes/No |
| Homosexuality declassified as a mental illness | Yes (since 1973) | Yes |
| Transgender identity declassified as a mental illness | Yes (since 1994) Not considered a mental illness.[^cite_note-774] | N/A |
| Intersex sex characteristics declassified as a physical deformity |  | Yes/No |
| [MSMs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Men_who_have_sex_with_men) allowed to donate blood | Yes[^cite_note-\:1-775] | Yes[^cite_note-\:1-775] |
| Female sex partners of [MSMs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Men_who_have_sex_with_men) allowed to donate blood | Yes/No (3-month deferral period) | N/A |

## Summary of state protections

[Employment protections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_cities_and_counties_in_the_United_States_offering_an_LGBT_non-discrimination_ordinance) will not be included on the following table, unless the state adopted them before 2020, as they have already been implemented nationwide under *Bostock v. Clayton County*.

| State Protections in Housing and Public Accommodations | Sexual orientation | Gender identity/Expression |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Alabama | No | No |
| Alaska | No | No |
| Arizona | No | No |
| Arkansas | No (LGBTQ Anti-Discrimination Ordinances covering [public accommodations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public_accommodations_in_the_United_States) and [housing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Housing_discrimination_in_the_United_States) are forbidden in Arkansas under the [Intrastate Commerce Improvement Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Intrastate_Commerce_Improvement_Act) signed in 2015) | No (LGBTQ Anti-Discrimination Ordinances covering [public accommodations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public_accommodations_in_the_United_States) and [housing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Housing_discrimination_in_the_United_States) are forbidden in Arkansas under the [Intrastate Commerce Improvement Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Intrastate_Commerce_Improvement_Act) signed in 2015) |
| California | Yes (Since 1992 in employment, since 2000 in housing, and since 2005 in public accommodations) | Yes (Since 2003 in employment and housing, and since 2005 in public accommodations) |
| Colorado | Yes (Since 2008 for [sexual orientation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sexuality) and [gender identity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gender_identity)) | Yes (Since 2008 for [sexual orientation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sexuality) and [gender identity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gender_identity)) |
| Connecticut | Yes (Since 1991) | Yes (Since 2011) |
| Delaware | Yes (Since 2009) | Yes (Since 2013) |
| Florida | (States Civil Rights Commission Implicitly Includes [sexuality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sexuality), but not state law) | (States Civil Rights Commission Implicitly Includes [gender identity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gender_identity), but not state law) |
| Georgia | No | No |
| Hawaii | Yes (Since 1991 for employment, since 2005 in housing, and since 2006 in public accommodations) | Yes (Since 2005 in housing, since 2006 in public accommodations, and since 2011 in employment) |
| Idaho | No | No |
| Illinois | Yes (Since 2006 for both sexuality and gender identity) | Yes (Since 2006 for both sexuality and gender identity) |
| Indiana | No | No |
| Iowa | Yes (Since 2007 for [sexuality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sexuality)) | No (Since 2007 ended 2025 for [gender identity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gender_identity) with the Iowa General Assembly repealing the clause from the state’s Civil Rights Act) |
| Kansas | (States Civil Rights Commission implicitly includes [sexuality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sexuality), but not state law) | (States Civil Rights Commission implicitly includes [gender identity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gender_identity), but not state law) |
| Kentucky | No | No |
| Louisiana | No | No |
| Maine | Yes (Since 2005 for both [sexuality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sexuality) and [gender identity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gender_identity)) | Yes (Since 2005 for both [sexuality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sexuality) and [gender identity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gender_identity)) |
| Maryland | Yes (Since 2001) | Yes (Since 2014) |
| Massachusetts | Yes (Since 1989) | Yes (Since 2016) |
| Michigan | Yes (Since 2019 for both [sexuality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sexuality) and [gender identity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gender_identity)) | Yes (Since 2019 for both [sexuality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sexuality) and [gender identity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gender_identity)) |
| Minnesota | Yes (Since 1993 for both [sexuality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sexuality) and [gender identity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gender_identity)) | Yes (Since 1993 for both [sexuality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sexuality) and [gender identity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gender_identity)) |
| Mississippi | No | No |
| Missouri | No | No |
| Montana | No | No |
| Nebraska | /No (States Civil Rights Commission implicitly includes [sexuality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sexuality) in housing protections, but not state law, and there are no protections in public accommodations) | /No (States Civil Rights Commission implicitly includes [gender identity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gender_identity) in housing protections, but not state law, and there are no protections in public accommodations) |
| Nevada | Yes (Since 1999 in employment, and since 2011 in all other areas) | Yes (Since 2011 in all areas) |
| New Hampshire | Yes (Since 1998) | Yes (Since 2019) |
| New Jersey | Yes (Since 1991) | Yes (Since 2006) |
| New Mexico | Yes (Since 2003 for both [sexuality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sexuality) and [gender identity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gender_identity)) | Yes (Since 2003 for both [sexuality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sexuality) and [gender identity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gender_identity)) |
| New York | Yes (Since 2003) | Yes (Since 2019) |
| North Carolina | No | No |
| North Dakota | No | No |
| Ohio | No | No |
| Oklahoma | No | No |
| Oregon | Yes (Since 2008 for both [sexuality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sexuality) and [gender identity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gender_identity)) | Yes (Since 2008 for both [sexuality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sexuality) and [gender identity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gender_identity)) |
| Pennsylvania | (States Civil Rights Commission implicitly includes [sexuality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sexuality) since 2019, but not state law) | (States Civil Rights Commission implicitly includes [gender identity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gender_identity) 2019, but not state law) |
| Rhode Island | Yes (Since 1995) | Yes (Since 2011) |
| South Carolina | No | No |
| South Dakota | No | No |
| Tennessee | No (LGBTQ Anti-discrimination ordinances covering [public accommodations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public_accommodations_in_the_United_States) and [housing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Housing_discrimination_in_the_United_States) are forbidden in Tennessee under the [Equal Access to Intrastate Commerce Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Equal_Access_to_Intrastate_Commerce_Act) signed in 2011) | No (LGBTQ Anti-discrimination ordinances covering [public accommodations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public_accommodations_in_the_United_States) and [housing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Housing_discrimination_in_the_United_States) are forbidden in Tennessee under the [Equal Access to Intrastate Commerce Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Equal_Access_to_Intrastate_Commerce_Act) signed in 2011) |
| Texas | No | No |
| Utah | Yes/No (Discrimination on the basis of [sexuality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sexuality) prohibited in only housing, and not public accommodations) | Yes/No (Discrimination on the basis of [gender identity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gender_identity) prohibited in only housing, and not public accommodations) |
| Vermont | Yes (Since 1992) | Yes (Since 2007) |
| Virginia | Yes (Since 2020 for both [sexuality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sexuality) and [gender identity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gender_identity)) | Yes (Since 2020 for both [sexuality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sexuality) and [gender identity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gender_identity)) |
| Washington | Yes (Since 2006 for both [sexuality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sexuality) and [gender identity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gender_identity)) | Yes (Since 2006 for both [sexuality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sexuality) and [gender identity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gender_identity)) |
| West Virginia | No | No |
| Wisconsin | Yes (Since 1982) | No |
| Wyoming | No | No |
